{"author":null,"body":"While reviewing the hook system for a production deployment, I noticed that `crates/tools/src/policy.rs` L106-L185 contains a function `resolve_effective_policy` that looks like an unfinished feature. Before I make assumptions about it in our own setup, I wanted to ask whether it's still the intended direction or abandoned scaffolding.\n\n## What it looks like\n\nThe function computes a layered `ToolPolicy` for a given `PolicyContext { agent_id, provider, channel, group_id, sender_id, sandboxed }`, merging allow/deny rules from six layers (in the function's documented order):\n\n1. Global — `tools.policy`\n2. Per-provider — `tools.providers.\u003cprovider\u003e.policy`\n3. Per-agent — `agents.list[i].tools.policy`\n4. Per-channel-group — `channels.\u003cch\u003e.groups.\u003cgid\u003e.tools.policy`\n5. Per-sender-in-group — `channels.\u003cch\u003e.groups.\u003cgid\u003e.tools.bySender.\u003csender\u003e`\n6. Sandbox — `tools.exec.sandbox.tools`\n\nDeny accumulates across layers; allow is replaced by later layers unless empty. It has full unit test coverage at `crates/tools/src/policy.rs` L257-L302, covering global / per-provider / deny-wins / prefix-pattern / merge behavior.\n\nThis is exactly the shape of policy system you'd want for per-channel and per-sender tool gating — the use case that motivated me to read this code in the first place.\n\n## Why I think it's unreachable today\n\nTwo problems:\n\n**1. No runtime callers.** I grep'd the entire workspace. `resolve_effective_policy` is only referenced by its own unit tests. The actual runtime tool-filtering path at `crates/chat/src/lib.rs` L1373-L1406 uses a much simpler function (`effective_tool_policy`) that reads only the global `config.tools.policy.allow/deny` — layers 2 through 6 are never consulted in production.\n\n**2. Schema drift.** The function takes `\u0026serde_json::Value` and walks JSON pointers like `/agents/list[i]/tools/policy` and `/channels/\u003cch\u003e/groups/\u003cgid\u003e/tools/policy`. But the current `MoltisConfig` schema doesn't have those shapes:\n\n- `AgentsConfig` at `crates/config/src/schema.rs` L252-L258 exposes `presets: HashMap\u003cString, AgentPreset\u003e` — there is no `agents.list` array, so layer 3's pointer never resolves.\n- `ChannelsConfig.telegram` / `.discord` / etc. at L1388-L1407 are `HashMap\u003cString, serde_json::Value\u003e` — opaque untyped blobs with no `groups.tools.policy` or `groups.tools.bySender` fields in the schema, so layers 4 and 5 never resolve.\n\nIn other words: even if something *did* call `resolve_effective_policy` today, only layers 1, 2, and 6 could ever match anything. Layers 3-5 — the interesting ones — are dead in both senses: no callers, and no config shape to read from.\n\n## My question\n\nIs this still the intended direction?\n\n- **If yes** — per-channel-group and per-sender tool policy is still on the roadmap — then it'd be great to know roughly what schema shape you'd want (typed `GroupToolPolicy` nested in `ChannelsConfig`? A separate `[policies]` section? Something else?). That would let folks like me plan around it rather than building a parallel mechanism in shell hooks that you'll later obsolete. I'd also be curious whether it's waiting on something specific (a refactor that hasn't landed, a design decision you're still sitting on, etc).\n\n- **If no** — this was explored and descoped — then it's probably worth deleting `resolve_effective_policy`, `PolicyContext`, and the associated tests so future readers don't mistake the scaffolding for intent. I'd be happy to hear \"we're not doing this, use a `BeforeToolCall` hook\" as an answer; it'd unblock our design decision in the other direction.\n\nEither answer helps. I just don't want to write 300 lines of shell-hook plumbing for a use case you're about to support natively, nor do I want to wait for a feature that's not coming.\n\n## Context for why I'm asking\n\nI'm setting up a MOLTIS-based autonomous agent that handles messages from multiple channels with varying trust levels (direct DMs from known users, group chats with mixed membership, cron-triggered runs that fetch external content). Per-channel and per-sender tool gating is the natural defense-in-depth layer I want. The `BeforeToolCall` hook gives me enforcement but no context about where the session came from — that's filed separately as #640 — and having `resolve_effective_policy` native would make the whole thing first-class instead of a shell-hook custom job.\n\nRelated issues I've filed in the same review pass: #631 (Whisper STT config), #632 (empty voice transcription), #633 (Discord attachments), #638 (ToolResultPersist not dispatched), #639 (MessageReceived read-only), #640 (channel metadata in hook payloads). They're all small, all complete scaffolding that already exists in the codebase. This one is the only architectural question — hence the Discussion rather than an Issue.","category":"Q\u0026A","comments":0,"created_at":"2026-04-10T12:01:35Z","title":"resolve_effective_policy is implemented with tests but unreachable — is this abandoned scaffolding or still planned?"}
```

     1→use {
     2→    serde::{Deserialize, Serialize},
     3→    tracing::debug,
     4→};
     5→
     6→/// Glob-based allow/deny policy for tool access.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8→pub struct ToolPolicy {
     9→    #[serde(default)]
    10→    pub allow: Vec<String>,
    11→    #[serde(default)]
    12→    pub deny: Vec<String>,
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Context for resolving which policy layers apply.
    16→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
    17→pub struct PolicyContext {
    18→    pub agent_id: String,
    19→    pub provider: Option<String>,
    20→    pub channel: Option<String>,
    21→    pub group_id: Option<String>,
    22→    pub sender_id: Option<String>,
    23→    pub sandboxed: bool,
    24→}
    25→
    26→/// Predefined tool profiles.
    27→pub fn profile_tools(profile: &str) -> ToolPolicy {
    28→    match profile {
    29→        "minimal" => ToolPolicy {
    30→            allow: vec!["exec".into()],
    31→            deny: Vec::new(),
    32→        },
    33→        "coding" => ToolPolicy {
    34→            allow: vec!["exec".into(), "browser".into(), "memory".into()],
    35→            deny: Vec::new(),
    36→        },
    37→        "full" => ToolPolicy {
    38→            allow: vec!["*".into()],
    39→            deny: Vec::new(),
    40→        },
    41→        _ => ToolPolicy::default(),
    42→    }
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Check if a tool name matches a glob pattern (supports `*` wildcard).
    46→fn pattern_matches(pattern: &str, name: &str) -> bool {
    47→    if pattern == "*" {
    48→        return true;
    49→    }
    50→    if let Some(prefix) = pattern.strip_suffix('*') {
    51→        return name.starts_with(prefix);
    52→    }
    53→    pattern == name
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl ToolPolicy {
    57→    /// Returns true if the given tool name is allowed by this policy.
    58→    /// Deny always wins over allow.
    59→    pub fn is_allowed(&self, tool_name: &str) -> bool {
    60→        // Check deny first — deny wins.
    61→        for pattern in &self.deny {
    62→            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {
    63→                return false;
    64→            }
    65→        }
    66→        // If allow is empty, everything not denied is allowed.
    67→        if self.allow.is_empty() {
    68→            return true;
    69→        }
    70→        // Otherwise, must match an allow pattern.
    71→        for pattern in &self.allow {
    72→            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {
    73→                return true;
    74→            }
    75→        }
    76→        false
    77→    }
    78→
    79→    /// Merge another policy on top of this one (the `other` has higher precedence).
    80→    /// Non-empty lists from `other` replace those from `self`.
    81→    pub fn merge_with(&self, other: &ToolPolicy) -> ToolPolicy {
    82→        ToolPolicy {
    83→            allow: if other.allow.is_empty() {
    84→                self.allow.clone()
    85→            } else {
    86→                other.allow.clone()
    87→            },
    88→            deny: {
    89→                let mut combined = self.deny.clone();
    90→                combined.extend(other.deny.iter().cloned());
    91→                combined
    92→            },
    93→        }
    94→    }
    95→}
    96→
    97→/// Resolve the effective policy by merging layers from config.
    98→///
    99→/// Layer precedence (later wins for allow, deny always accumulates):
   100→/// 1. Global
   101→/// 2. Per-provider
   102→/// 3. Per-agent
   103→/// 4. Per-group
   104→/// 5. Per-sender in group
   105→/// 6. Sandbox-specific
   106→pub fn resolve_effective_policy(config: &serde_json::Value, context: &PolicyContext) -> ToolPolicy {
   107→    let mut effective = ToolPolicy::default();
   108→
   109→    // Layer 1: Global — tools.policy
   110→    if let Some(global) = config.pointer("/tools/policy")
   111→        && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(global.clone())
   112→    {
   113→        effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   114→        debug!("policy: applied global layer");
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    // Layer 2: Per-provider — tools.providers.<provider>.policy
   118→    if let Some(ref provider) = context.provider {
   119→        let pointer = format!("/tools/providers/{}/policy", provider);
   120→        if let Some(prov) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   121→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(prov.clone())
   122→        {
   123→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   124→            debug!(provider, "policy: applied provider layer");
   125→        }
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    // Layer 3: Per-agent — agents.list[agent_id].tools.policy
   129→    // We scan the agents list for a matching agent_id.
   130→    if let Some(agents) = config.pointer("/agents/list").and_then(|v| v.as_array()) {
   131→        for agent in agents {
   132→            let id = agent.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
   133→            if id == context.agent_id {
   134→                if let Some(agent_policy) = agent.pointer("/tools/policy")
   135→                    && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(agent_policy.clone())
   136→                {
   137→                    effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   138→                    debug!(agent_id = %context.agent_id, "policy: applied agent layer");
   139→                }
   140→                break;
   141→            }
   142→        }
   143→    }
   144→
   145→    // Layer 4: Per-group — channels.<ch>.groups.<gid>.tools.policy
   146→    if let Some(ref channel) = context.channel
   147→        && let Some(ref group_id) = context.group_id
   148→    {
   149→        let pointer = format!("/channels/{}/groups/{}/tools/policy", channel, group_id);
   150→        if let Some(group) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   151→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(group.clone())
   152→        {
   153→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   154→            debug!(channel, group_id, "policy: applied group layer");
   155→        }
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    // Layer 5: Per-sender — channels.<ch>.groups.<gid>.tools.bySender.<sender>
   159→    if let Some(ref channel) = context.channel
   160→        && let Some(ref group_id) = context.group_id
   161→        && let Some(ref sender_id) = context.sender_id
   162→    {
   163→        let pointer = format!(
   164→            "/channels/{}/groups/{}/tools/bySender/{}",
   165→            channel, group_id, sender_id
   166→        );
   167→        if let Some(sender) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   168→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(sender.clone())
   169→        {
   170→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   171→            debug!(channel, group_id, sender_id, "policy: applied sender layer");
   172→        }
   173→    }
   174→
   175→    // Layer 6: Sandbox overrides
   176→    if context.sandboxed
   177→        && let Some(sandbox_policy) = config.pointer("/tools/exec/sandbox/tools")
   178→        && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(sandbox_policy.clone())
   179→    {
   180→        effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   181→        debug!("policy: applied sandbox layer");
   182→    }
   183→
   184→    effective
   185→}
   186→
   187→#[cfg(test)]
   188→mod tests {
   189→    use super::*;
   190→
   191→    #[test]
   192→    fn test_allow_all() {
   193→        let policy = ToolPolicy {
   194→            allow: vec!["*".into()],
   195→            deny: Vec::new(),
   196→        };
   197→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   198→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser"));
   199→    }
   200→
   201→    #[test]
   202→    fn test_deny_wins() {
   203→        let policy = ToolPolicy {
   204→            allow: vec!["*".into()],
   205→            deny: vec!["exec".into()],
   206→        };
   207→        assert!(!policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   208→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser"));
   209→    }
   210→
   211→    #[test]
   212→    fn test_prefix_pattern() {
   213→        let policy = ToolPolicy {
   214→            allow: vec!["browser*".into()],
   215→            deny: Vec::new(),
   216→        };
   217→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser"));
   218→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser.fetch"));
   219→        assert!(!policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   220→    }
   221→
   222→    #[test]
   223→    fn test_empty_allow_permits_all() {
   224→        let policy = ToolPolicy {
   225→            allow: Vec::new(),
   226→            deny: Vec::new(),
   227→        };
   228→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   229→    }
   230→
   231→    #[test]
   232→    fn test_merge_deny_accumulates() {
   233→        let base = ToolPolicy {

   235→            deny: vec!["dangerous".into()],
   236→        };
   237→        let overlay = ToolPolicy {

   241→        let merged = base.merge_with(&overlay);
   242→        assert!(!merged.is_allowed("dangerous"));
   243→        assert!(!merged.is_allowed("exec"));
   244→        assert!(merged.is_allowed("browser"));

   246→
   247→    #[test]

   250→        assert!(minimal.is_allowed("exec"));
   251→        assert!(!minimal.is_allowed("browser"));
   252→
   253→        let full = profile_tools("full");

   256→

> AGENT

The discussion author's analysis is **accurate on all counts**. Here's what the code confirms:

1. **`resolve_effective_policy` is unreachable** — it exists only in `crates/tools/src/policy.rs` and is called exclusively by its own unit tests. The actual runtime path at `crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1683` uses a separate `effective_tool_policy()` that reads only global config (`config.tools.policy.allow/deny` + optional profile).

2. **Schema drift is real** — the function walks JSON pointers like `/agents/list[i]`, but `AgentsConfig` uses `presets: HashMap<String, AgentPreset>` (not an array). Channel configs are `HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>` with no `groups`, `tools.policy`, or `bySender` substructure. So layers 3–5 can never resolve even if the function were called.

3. **Only layers 1, 2, and 6 could theoretically work** — global policy, per-provider, and sandbox. But even those aren't wired in; the runtime uses a completely different code path.

**My take:** This is classic "designed ahead of implementation" scaffolding. The layered merge logic and the `PolicyContext` struct show clear intent — someone designed a proper defense-in-depth policy system — but it was never connected to either the config schema or the runtime. The discussion author is right that it's in a dead zone: too well-tested to look like an accident, too disconnected to be functional.

The practical options are:

- **Ship it properly**: wire `resolve_effective_policy` into the runtime path (replacing `effective_tool_policy`), extend `AgentsConfig` and `ChannelsConfig` with typed policy fields, and add the `PolicyContext` to the chat service call site. This would be a meaningful feature for multi-channel deployments.

- **Remove it**: delete `resolve_effective_policy`, `PolicyContext`, and the associated tests. Point users to `BeforeToolCall` hooks (once #640 lands with channel metadata in hook payloads).

The author's use case — per-channel/per-sender tool gating for mixed-trust environments — is legitimate and this is exactly the kind of thing that should be first-class rather than reimplemented in shell hooks. If it's on the roadmap, even a rough timeline would let people like this avoid building parallel mechanisms.

Want me to create a beads issue to track making a decision on this, or draft a response to the discussion?

```
The `SandboxConfig` does not have a `tools` field. The sandbox layer (layer 6) in the original `resolve_effective_policy` reads `tools.exec.sandbox.tools` which does not exist in the schema. This layer should probably be either:
- Added as a new `ToolPolicyConfig` field on `SandboxConfig` (for restricting tools when sandboxed), or
- Removed as unused (since it has never been wired up)

Given the intent, adding a `tools_policy` field to `SandboxConfig` makes sense.

**Update `PolicyContext`**: Add `channel_account_id` since the channel config is keyed by `(channel_type, account_id)`.

```rust
pub struct PolicyContext {
    pub agent_id: String,
    pub provider: Option<String>,
    pub channel: Option<String>,          // channel_type
    pub channel_account_id: Option<String>, // NEW
    pub group_id: Option<String>,          // chat_type e.g. "group", "private"
    pub sender_id: Option<String>,         // peer_id
    pub sandboxed: bool,
}
```

---

#### 3. `crates/chat/src/lib.rs` -- Wire into Runtime

**3a. Replace `effective_tool_policy`** (line 1683) and update `apply_runtime_tool_filters` (line 1697):

The `apply_runtime_tool_filters` function currently takes `config: &MoltisConfig` but only passes it to `effective_tool_policy`. Change it to also accept a `PolicyContext` and call `resolve_effective_policy`:

```rust
fn apply_runtime_tool_filters(
    base: &ToolRegistry,
    config: &moltis_config::MoltisConfig,
    _skills: &[moltis_skills::types::SkillMetadata],
    mcp_disabled: bool,
    policy_context: &PolicyContext,  // NEW parameter
) -> ToolRegistry {
    let base_registry = if mcp_disabled {
        base.clone_without_mcp()
    } else {
        base.clone_without(&[])
    };

    let policy = resolve_effective_policy(config, policy_context);
    base_registry.clone_allowed_by(|name| policy.is_allowed(name))
}
```

**3b. Build `PolicyContext` at each call site**

There are 4 call sites for `apply_runtime_tool_filters`:

1. **Line 5195** (`list_tools` / schema listing) -- This is a simple listing; use a minimal context with just `agent_id = "main"`.

2. **Line 5402** (non-streaming send) -- The runtime context is available in `runtime_context` which has `channel_type`, `channel_account_id`, `channel_chat_type`. The session entry has `agent_id`. The `sender_id` needs to come from the `params`.

3. **Line 5525** (streaming send) -- Same as above.

4. **Line 6654** (the inner `run_streaming_agent_turn`) -- This receives `session_key`, `agent_id`, and the `runtime_context` has channel info.

For building the context at each call site:

```rust
let policy_context = PolicyContext {
    agent_id: agent_id.clone(),
    provider: runtime_context.as_ref()
        .and_then(|rc| rc.host.provider.clone()),
    channel: runtime_context.as_ref()
        .and_then(|rc| rc.host.channel_type.clone()),
    channel_account_id: runtime_context.as_ref()
        .and_then(|rc| rc.host.channel_account_id.clone()),
    group_id: runtime_context.as_ref()
        .and_then(|rc| rc.host.channel_chat_type.clone()),
    sender_id: params.get("_sender_id")
        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
        .map(String::from),
    sandboxed: session_is_sandboxed,
};
```

**3c. Thread `sender_id` through the channel dispatch path**

Currently, `ChannelMessageMeta` does not carry `peer_id` (the sender's platform ID). The `dispatch_to_chat` implementation in `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs` builds `params` as:
```rust
let mut params = serde_json::json!({
    "text": effective_text,
    "channel": &meta,
    "_session_key": &session_key,
    "_channel_reply_target": &reply_to,
});
```

We need to add `peer_id` to either `ChannelMessageMeta` or directly to `params`:

Option A (recommended): Add `sender_id: Option<String>` to `ChannelMessageMeta` and populate it from `ChannelEvent::InboundMessage.peer_id` in the dispatch implementation.

Then in `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs` line ~311:
```rust
params["_sender_id"] = serde_json::json!(&meta.sender_id);
```

In `crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, extract from params when building `PolicyContext`.

---

#### 4. `crates/config/src/validate.rs` -- Validation Updates

**4a. Update `build_schema_map()` (around line 442)**

The `channels` entry currently uses `Map(Box::new(Leaf))` for each channel type value. This needs to become more specific to recognize the `tools` key inside channel account configs:

```rust
let channel_account_tools = || {
    Struct(HashMap::from([
        ("groups", Map(Box::new(Struct(HashMap::from([
            ("policy", Struct(HashMap::from([
                ("allow", Leaf),
                ("deny", Leaf),
                ("profile", Leaf),
            ]))),
            ("by_sender", Map(Box::new(Struct(HashMap::from([
                ("allow", Leaf),
                ("deny", Leaf),
                ("profile", Leaf),
            ]))))),
        ]))))),
    ]))
};

let channel_account = || {
    MapWithFields {
        value: Box::new(Leaf),
        fields: HashMap::from([
            ("tools", channel_account_tools()),
            // other known channel fields could go here
        ]),
    }
};
```

Then update the channel type entries:
```rust
("telegram", Map(Box::new(channel_account()))),
```

Add to `provider_entry`:

("policy", Struct(HashMap::from([
    ("allow", Leaf),
    ("deny", Leaf),
    ("profile", Leaf),
]))),
```

**4c. Update `build_schema_map()` for sandbox `tools_policy`** (around line 148):

Add to `sandbox()`:

    ("allow", Leaf),

    ("profile", Leaf),
]))),
```

- Warn if channel tool policy references a non-existent group type
- Warn if provider policy is set but the provider is disabled

Under `[providers.anthropic]` section:

```

    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]

#### 7. Tests

**7a. Update existing tests in `crates/tools/src/policy.rs`**:
- Refactor tests to construct `MoltisConfig` instead of raw JSON
- Test all 6 layers with typed config
- Test merge precedence (deny accumulates, allow replaces)

**7b. Add tests in `crates/config/src/schema.rs`**:
- Deserialize TOML with channel tool policy overrides
- Verify `tool_policy_for_account` extraction

**7c. Add tests in `crates/chat/src/lib.rs`**:
- Test `apply_runtime_tool_filters` with full `PolicyContext`
- Test that channel sender restrictions work end-to-end

**7d. Update `crates/config/src/validate.rs` tests**:
- Verify new keys are recognized and don't produce "unknown key" warnings

---

### Implementation Sequence

1. **Schema changes** (`schema.rs`): Add `ToolPolicyConfig` to `ProviderEntry`, `ChannelToolPolicyOverride`/`GroupToolPolicy` structs, `tools_policy` to `SandboxConfig`, export new types from `lib.rs`
2. **Channel metadata** (`plugin.rs`): Add `sender_id` to `ChannelMessageMeta`
3. **Channel dispatch** (`channel_events.rs`): Populate `sender_id` in meta from `peer_id`, thread `_sender_id` into params
4. **Policy refactor** (`policy.rs`): Change signature to typed config, fix all 6 layers, update `PolicyContext`
5. **Runtime wiring** (`lib.rs`): Update `apply_runtime_tool_filters` signature, build `PolicyContext` at all 4 call sites
6. **Validation** (`validate.rs`): Update `build_schema_map()` for new fields
7. **Template** (`template.rs`): Add commented examples
8. **Tests**: All files above

### Potential Challenges

1. **Channel config is `serde_json::Value`**: Since channel accounts are stored as raw JSON in the typed config, extracting the `tools` sub-object requires careful deserialization. The `tool_policy_for_account` helper isolates this complexity.

2. **`sender_id` availability**: For web UI messages, there is no `sender_id` -- this is fine, layers 5 just won't apply. For channel messages, the `peer_id` from `ChannelEvent::InboundMessage` needs to be threaded through `ChannelMessageMeta` and then into `params["_sender_id"]`.

3. **`group_id` semantics**: In the current design, `group_id` maps to `channel_chat_type` (e.g., "group", "private", "channel"). This is the natural grouping -- you want different policies for group chats vs. DMs. This should be clearly documented.

4. **Agent ID for main session**: The main session agent ID is typically `"main"`. The preset lookup `config.agents.presets.get("main")` would need a preset named `"main"` to apply. This is a deliberate design: if you want per-agent tool policy for the main session, you create a preset named `"main"` (or whatever the session's `agent_id` is). The existing doc comments already note that presets only apply to sub-agents. This layer should be documented as applying to the agent identified in `PolicyContext.agent_id`, which for the main session means it only activates if a matching preset exists. Alternatively, the layer 3 comment could be clarified that for the main session, `tools.policy` (layer 1) is the right place.

5. **Backward compatibility**: The old `resolve_effective_policy` was never called from production code (only its own tests), so changing its signature is safe. The existing `effective_tool_policy` behavior is preserved as layer 1 in the new implementation.

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/tools/src/policy.rs` - Core policy resolution logic: refactor from raw JSON to typed config, fix all 6 layers
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/config/src/schema.rs` - Schema changes: add policy fields to ProviderEntry, SandboxConfig; add ChannelToolPolicyOverride/GroupToolPolicy structs
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/chat/src/lib.rs` - Runtime wiring: update apply_runtime_tool_filters to accept PolicyContext, build context at all 4 call sites
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/config/src/validate.rs` - Validation: update build_schema_map() for new provider.policy, channel tools, and sandbox tools_policy fields
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs` - Channel dispatch: thread peer_id as sender_id through params for PolicyContext construction

    10→// ── Reasoning effort ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    11→
    12→/// Reasoning/thinking effort level for models that support extended thinking.
    13→///
    14→/// Maps to provider-specific parameters:
    15→/// - **Anthropic**: `thinking.budget_tokens` (low=4096, medium=10240, high=32768)
    16→/// - **OpenAI**: `reasoning_effort` field on o-series models
    17→/// - **Other providers**: ignored if unsupported
    18→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    19→#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
    20→pub enum ReasoningEffort {
    21→    Low,
    22→    Medium,
    23→    High,
    24→}
    25→
    26→/// Agent identity (name, emoji, theme).
    27→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    28→#[serde(default)]
    29→pub struct AgentIdentity {
    30→    pub name: Option<String>,
    31→    pub emoji: Option<String>,
    32→    pub theme: Option<String>,
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// IANA timezone (e.g. `"Europe/Paris"`).
    36→///
    37→/// Wraps [`chrono_tz::Tz`] and (de)serialises as a plain string so it stays
    38→/// compatible with the YAML frontmatter in `USER.md`.
    39→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    40→pub struct Timezone(pub chrono_tz::Tz);
    41→
    42→#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
    43→#[error("unknown IANA timezone: {value}")]
    44→pub struct TimezoneParseError {
    45→    value: String,
    46→}
    47→
    48→impl Timezone {
    49→    /// The IANA name, e.g. `"Europe/Paris"`.
    50→    #[must_use]
    51→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    52→        self.0.name()
    53→    }
    54→
    55→    /// The inner [`chrono_tz::Tz`] value.
    56→    #[must_use]
    57→    pub fn tz(&self) -> chrono_tz::Tz {
    58→        self.0
    59→    }
    60→}
    61→
    62→impl std::fmt::Display for Timezone {
    63→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    64→        f.write_str(self.0.name())
    65→    }
    66→}
    67→
    68→impl std::str::FromStr for Timezone {
    69→    type Err = TimezoneParseError;
    70→
    71→    fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
    72→        s.parse::<chrono_tz::Tz>()
    73→            .map(Self)
    74→            .map_err(|_| TimezoneParseError {
    75→                value: s.to_string(),
    76→            })
    77→    }
    78→}
    79→
    80→impl From<chrono_tz::Tz> for Timezone {
    81→    fn from(tz: chrono_tz::Tz) -> Self {
    82→        Self(tz)
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→impl Serialize for Timezone {
    87→    fn serialize<S: serde::Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {
    88→        serializer.serialize_str(self.0.name())
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Timezone {
    93→    fn deserialize<D: Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {
    94→        let s = String::deserialize(deserializer)?;
    95→        s.parse::<Self>().map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
    96→    }
    97→}
    98→
    99→/// Geographic coordinates (WGS 84).
   100→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]

     1→//! Configuration validation engine.
     2→//!
     3→//! Validates TOML configuration files against the known schema, detects
     4→//! unknown/misspelled fields, and reports security warnings.
     5→
     6→use std::{collections::HashMap, path::Path};
     7→
     8→use secrecy::ExposeSecret;
     9→
    10→use crate::schema::MoltisConfig;
    11→
    12→/// Severity level for a diagnostic.
    13→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    14→pub enum Severity {
    15→    Error,
    16→    Warning,
    17→    Info,
    18→}
    19→
    20→impl std::fmt::Display for Severity {
    21→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    22→        match self {
    23→            Self::Error => write!(f, "error"),
    24→            Self::Warning => write!(f, "warning"),
    25→            Self::Info => write!(f, "info"),
    26→        }
    27→    }
    28→}
    29→
    30→/// A single validation diagnostic.
    31→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    32→pub struct Diagnostic {
    33→    pub severity: Severity,
    34→    /// Category: "syntax", "unknown-field", "deprecated-field", "unknown-provider", "type-error",
    35→    /// "security", "file-ref"
    36→    pub category: &'static str,
    37→    /// Dotted path, e.g. "server.bnd"
    38→    pub path: String,
    39→    pub message: String,
    40→}
    41→
    42→/// Result of validating a configuration file.
    43→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    44→pub struct ValidationResult {
    45→    pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    46→    pub config_path: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
    47→}
    48→
    49→impl ValidationResult {
    50→    /// Returns `true` if any diagnostic is an error.
    51→    #[must_use]
    52→    pub fn has_errors(&self) -> bool {
    53→        self.diagnostics
    54→            .iter()
    55→            .any(|d| d.severity == Severity::Error)
    56→    }
    57→
    58→    /// Count diagnostics by severity.
    59→    #[must_use]
    60→    pub fn count(&self, severity: Severity) -> usize {
    61→        self.diagnostics
    62→            .iter()
    63→            .filter(|d| d.severity == severity)
    64→            .count()
    65→    }
    66→}
    67→
    68→// ── Schema tree for unknown-field detection ─────────────────────────────────
    69→
    70→/// Represents the expected shape of the configuration schema.
    71→enum KnownKeys {
    72→    /// A struct with fixed field names.
    73→    Struct(HashMap<&'static str, KnownKeys>),
    74→    /// A map with dynamic keys (providers, mcp.servers, etc.) whose values
    75→    /// have a known shape.
    76→    Map(Box<KnownKeys>),
    77→    /// A map with dynamic keys plus explicit static keys.

```
   180→    async fn dispatch_to_chat(
   181→        &self,
   182→        text: &str,
   183→        reply_to: ChannelReplyTarget,
   184→        meta: ChannelMessageMeta,
   185→    ) {
   186→        if let Some(state) = self.state.get() {
   187→            // Start typing immediately so pre-run setup (session/model resolution)
   188→            // does not delay channel feedback.
   189→            let typing_done = start_channel_typing_loop(state, &reply_to);
   190→
   191→            let session_key = if let Some(ref sm) = state.services.session_metadata {
   192→                resolve_channel_session(&reply_to, sm).await
   193→            } else {
   194→                default_channel_session_key(&reply_to)
   195→            };
   196→            let effective_text = if state.is_channel_command_mode_enabled(&session_key).await {
   197→                rewrite_for_shell_mode(text).unwrap_or_else(|| text.to_string())
   198→            } else {
   199→                text.to_string()
   200→            };
   201→
   202→            // Broadcast a "chat" event so the web UI shows the user message
   203→            // in real-time (like typing from the UI).
   204→            //
   205→            // We intentionally omit `messageIndex` here: the broadcast fires
   206→            // *before* chat.send() persists the message, so store.count()
   207→            // would be stale.  Concurrent channel messages would get the same
   208→            // index, causing the client-side dedup to drop the second one.
   209→            // Without a messageIndex the client skips its dedup check and
   210→            // always renders the message.
   211→            let payload = serde_json::json!({
   212→                "state": "channel_user",
   213→                "text": text,
   214→                "channel": &meta,
   215→                "sessionKey": &session_key,
   216→            });
   217→            broadcast(state, "chat", payload, BroadcastOpts {
   218→                drop_if_slow: true,
   219→                ..Default::default()
   220→            })
   221→            .await;
   222→
   223→            // Persist channel binding so web UI messages on this session
   224→            // can be echoed back to the channel.
   225→            if let Ok(binding_json) = serde_json::to_string(&reply_to)
   226→                && let Some(ref session_meta) = state.services.session_metadata
   227→            {
   228→                // Ensure the session row exists and label it on first use.
   229→                // `set_channel_binding` is an UPDATE, so the row must exist
   230→                // before we can set the binding column.
   231→                let entry = session_meta.get(&session_key).await;
   232→                if entry.as_ref().is_none_or(|e| e.channel_binding.is_none()) {
   233→                    let existing = session_meta
   234→                        .list_channel_sessions(
   235→                            reply_to.channel_type.as_str(),
   236→                            &reply_to.account_id,
   237→                            &reply_to.chat_id,
   238→                        )
   239→                        .await;
   240→                    let n = existing.len() + 1;
   241→                    let _ = session_meta
   242→                        .upsert(
   243→                            &session_key,
   244→                            Some(format!("{} {n}", reply_to.channel_type.display_name())),
   245→                        )
   246→                        .await;
   247→                }
   248→                session_meta
   249→                    .set_channel_binding(&session_key, Some(binding_json))
   250→                    .await;
   251→                if let Some(entry) = session_meta.get(&session_key).await
   252→                    && entry
   253→                        .agent_id
   254→                        .as_deref()
   255→                        .map(str::trim)
   256→                        .is_none_or(|value| value.is_empty())
   257→                {
   258→                    let default_agent = if let Some(ref store) = state.services.agent_persona_store
   259→                    {
   260→                        store
   261→                            .default_id()
   262→                            .await
   263→                            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "main".to_string())
   264→                    } else {
   265→                        "main".to_string()
   266→                    };
   267→                    let _ = session_meta
   268→                        .set_agent_id(&session_key, Some(&default_agent))
   269→                        .await;
   270→                }
   271→            }
   272→
   273→            // Channel platforms do not expose bot read receipts. Use inbound
   274→            // user activity as a heuristic and mark prior session history seen.
   275→            state.services.session.mark_seen(&session_key).await;
   276→
   277→            // If the message is a thread reply, fetch prior thread messages
   278→            // for context injection so the LLM sees the conversation history.
   279→            let thread_context = if let Some(ref thread_id) = reply_to.message_id
   280→                && let Some(ref reg) = state.services.channel_registry
   281→            {
   282→                match reg
   283→                    .fetch_thread_messages(&reply_to.account_id, &reply_to.chat_id, thread_id, 20)
   284→                    .await
   285→                {
   286→                    Ok(msgs) if !msgs.is_empty() => {
   287→                        let history: Vec<serde_json::Value> = msgs
   288→                            .iter()
   289→                            .map(|m| {
   290→                                serde_json::json!({
   291→                                    "role": if m.is_bot { "assistant" } else { "user" },
   292→                                    "text": m.text,
   293→                                    "sender_id": m.sender_id,
   294→                                    "timestamp": m.timestamp,
   295→                                })
   296→                            })
   297→                            .collect();
   298→                        Some(history)
   299→                    },

   342→                };

   353→
   354→                    // Buffer model notification for the logbook instead of sending separately.
   355→                    let display: String = if let Ok(models_val) = state.services.model.list().await

   359→                            .iter()

   392→                    // Buffer model notification for the logbook.

   396→                        .unwrap_or(id);
   397→                    let msg = format!("Using {display}. Use /model to change.");
   398→                    state.push_channel_status_log(&session_key, msg).await;
   399→                }
   400→            }
   401→
   402→            let send_result = chat.send(params).await;
   403→            if let Some(done_tx) = typing_done {
   404→                let _ = done_tx.send(());
   405→            }
   406→
   407→            if let Err(e) = send_result {
   408→                error!("channel dispatch_to_chat failed: {e}");

   411→                if let Some(outbound) = state.services.channel_outbound_arc() {

   418→                            reply_to.message_id.as_deref(),
   419→                        )

Has no effect on\n    /// providers with automatic server-side caching (OpenAI, DeepSeek, Ollama).\n    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = \"is_default_cache_retention\")]\n    pub cache_retention: CacheRetention,\n\n    /// Tool policy override for this provider.   "old_string": "/// Channel configuration.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(default)]\npub struct ChannelsConfig {",
  "new_string": "/// Per-chat-type tool policy for a channel account.\n///\n/// Keyed by chat type (e.g. `\"private\"`, `\"group\"`, `\"channel\"`).\n/// Each entry can have an allow/deny policy and per-sender overrides.\n///\n/// Example TOML:\n/// ```toml\n/// [channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]\n/// deny = [\"exec\"]\n///\n/// [channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender]\n/// \"123456\" = { allow = [\"*\"], deny = [] }\n/// ```\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(default)]\npub struct GroupToolPolicy {\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub allow: Vec<String>,\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub deny: Vec<String>,\n    /// Per-sender overrides within this group, keyed by sender/peer ID.\n    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = \"HashMap::is_empty\")]\n    pub by_sender: HashMap<String, ToolPolicyConfig>,\n}\n\n/// Tool policy overrides for a channel account.\n///\n/// Lives at `channels.<type>.<account_id>.tools` in the config.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(default)]\npub struct ChannelToolPolicyOverride {\n    /// Per-chat-type policies, keyed by chat type (`\"private\"`, `\"group\"`, etc.).\n    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = \"HashMap::is_empty\")]\n    pub groups: HashMap<String, GroupToolPolicy>,\n}\n\n/// Channel configuration.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(default)]\npub struct ChannelsConfig {"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/config/src/schema.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Iterate all channel configs (named + extra) as `(channel_type, accounts)` pairs.\n    pub fn all_channel_configs(&self) -> Vec<(&str, &HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>)> {\n        let mut v: Vec<(&str, &HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>)> =\n            self.named_fields().into_iter().collect();\n        for (ct, accounts) in &self.extra {\n            v.push((ct.as_str(), accounts));\n        }\n        v\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// Iterate all channel configs (named + extra) as `(channel_type, accounts)` pairs.\n    pub fn all_channel_configs(&self) -> Vec<(&str, &HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>)> {\n        let mut v: Vec<(&str, &HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>)> =\n            self.named_fields().into_iter().collect();\n        for (ct, accounts) in &self.extra {\n            v.push((ct.as_str(), accounts));\n        }\n        v\n    }\n\n    /// Extract the `tools` sub-object for a specific channel account.\n    ///\n    /// Channel accounts are stored as `serde_json::Value`, so we deserialize\n    /// just the `tools` key on demand.\n    pub fn tool_policy_for_account(\n        &self,\n        channel_type: &str,\n        account_id: &str,\n    ) -> Option<ChannelToolPolicyOverride> {\n        let accounts = self\n            .all_channel_configs()\n            .into_iter()\n            .find(|(ct, _)| *ct == channel_type)\n            .map(|(_, accounts)| accounts)?;\n        let account_val = accounts.get(account_id)?;\n        let tools_val = account_val.get(\"tools\")?;\n        serde_json::from_value::<ChannelToolPolicyOverride>(tools_val.clone()).ok()\n    }\n}"
}

     1→use {
     2→    serde::{Deserialize, Serialize},
     3→    tracing::debug,
     4→};
     5→
     6→/// Glob-based allow/deny policy for tool access.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8→pub struct ToolPolicy {
     9→    #[serde(default)]
    10→    pub allow: Vec<String>,
    11→    #[serde(default)]
    12→    pub deny: Vec<String>,
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Context for resolving which policy layers apply.
    16→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
    17→pub struct PolicyContext {
    18→    pub agent_id: String,
    19→    pub provider: Option<String>,
    20→    pub channel: Option<String>,
    21→    pub group_id: Option<String>,
    22→    pub sender_id: Option<String>,
    23→    pub sandboxed: bool,
    24→}
    25→
    26→/// Predefined tool profiles.
    27→pub fn profile_tools(profile: &str) -> ToolPolicy {
    28→    match profile {
    29→        "minimal" => ToolPolicy {
    30→            allow: vec!["exec".into()],
    31→            deny: Vec::new(),
    32→        },
    33→        "coding" => ToolPolicy {
    34→            allow: vec!["exec".into(), "browser".into(), "memory".into()],
    35→            deny: Vec::new(),
    36→        },
    37→        "full" => ToolPolicy {
    38→            allow: vec!["*".into()],
    39→            deny: Vec::new(),
    40→        },
    41→        _ => ToolPolicy::default(),
    42→    }
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Check if a tool name matches a glob pattern (supports `*` wildcard).
    46→fn pattern_matches(pattern: &str, name: &str) -> bool {
    47→    if pattern == "*" {
    48→        return true;
    49→    }
    50→    if let Some(prefix) = pattern.strip_suffix('*') {
    51→        return name.starts_with(prefix);
    52→    }
    53→    pattern == name
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl ToolPolicy {
    57→    /// Returns true if the given tool name is allowed by this policy.
    58→    /// Deny always wins over allow.
    59→    pub fn is_allowed(&self, tool_name: &str) -> bool {
    60→        // Check deny first — deny wins.
    61→        for pattern in &self.deny {
    62→            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {
    63→                return false;
    64→            }
    65→        }
    66→        // If allow is empty, everything not denied is allowed.
    67→        if self.allow.is_empty() {
    68→            return true;
    69→        }
    70→        // Otherwise, must match an allow pattern.
    71→        for pattern in &self.allow {
    72→            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {
    73→                return true;
    74→            }
    75→        }
    76→        false
    77→    }
    78→
    79→    /// Merge another policy on top of this one (the `other` has higher precedence).
    80→    /// Non-empty lists from `other` replace those from `self`.
    81→    pub fn merge_with(&self, other: &ToolPolicy) -> ToolPolicy {
    82→        ToolPolicy {
    83→            allow: if other.allow.is_empty() {
    84→                self.allow.clone()
    85→            } else {
    86→                other.allow.clone()
    87→            },
    88→            deny: {
    89→                let mut combined = self.deny.clone();
    90→                combined.extend(other.deny.iter().cloned());
    91→                combined
    92→            },
    93→        }
    94→    }
    95→}
    96→
    97→/// Resolve the effective policy by merging layers from config.
    98→///
    99→/// Layer precedence (later wins for allow, deny always accumulates):
   100→/// 1. Global
   101→/// 2. Per-provider
   102→/// 3. Per-agent
   103→/// 4. Per-group
   104→/// 5. Per-sender in group
   105→/// 6. Sandbox-specific
   106→pub fn resolve_effective_policy(config: &serde_json::Value, context: &PolicyContext) -> ToolPolicy {
   107→    let mut effective = ToolPolicy::default();
   108→
   109→    // Layer 1: Global — tools.policy
   110→    if let Some(global) = config.pointer("/tools/policy")
   111→        && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(global.clone())
   112→    {
   113→        effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   114→        debug!("policy: applied global layer");
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    // Layer 2: Per-provider — tools.providers.<provider>.policy
   118→    if let Some(ref provider) = context.provider {
   119→        let pointer = format!("/tools/providers/{}/policy", provider);
   120→        if let Some(prov) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   121→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(prov.clone())
   122→        {
   123→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   124→            debug!(provider, "policy: applied provider layer");
   125→        }
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    // Layer 3: Per-agent — agents.list[agent_id].tools.policy
   129→    // We scan the agents list for a matching agent_id.
   130→    if let Some(agents) = config.pointer("/agents/list").and_then(|v| v.as_array()) {
   131→        for agent in agents {
   132→            let id = agent.get("id").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
   133→            if id == context.agent_id {
   134→                if let Some(agent_policy) = agent.pointer("/tools/policy")
   135→                    && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(agent_policy.clone())
   136→                {
   137→                    effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   138→                    debug!(agent_id = %context.agent_id, "policy: applied agent layer");
   139→                }
   140→                break;
   141→            }
   142→        }
   143→    }
   144→
   145→    // Layer 4: Per-group — channels.<ch>.groups.<gid>.tools.policy
   146→    if let Some(ref channel) = context.channel
   147→        && let Some(ref group_id) = context.group_id
   148→    {
   149→        let pointer = format!("/channels/{}/groups/{}/tools/policy", channel, group_id);
   150→        if let Some(group) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   151→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(group.clone())
   152→        {
   153→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   154→            debug!(channel, group_id, "policy: applied group layer");
   155→        }
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    // Layer 5: Per-sender — channels.<ch>.groups.<gid>.tools.bySender.<sender>
   159→    if let Some(ref channel) = context.channel
   160→        && let Some(ref group_id) = context.group_id
   161→        && let Some(ref sender_id) = context.sender_id
   162→    {
   163→        let pointer = format!(
   164→            "/channels/{}/groups/{}/tools/bySender/{}",
   165→            channel, group_id, sender_id
   166→        );
   167→        if let Some(sender) = config.pointer(&pointer)
   168→            && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(sender.clone())
   169→        {
   170→            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   171→            debug!(channel, group_id, sender_id, "policy: applied sender layer");
   172→        }
   173→    }
   174→
   175→    // Layer 6: Sandbox overrides
   176→    if context.sandboxed
   177→        && let Some(sandbox_policy) = config.pointer("/tools/exec/sandbox/tools")
   178→        && let Ok(p) = serde_json::from_value::<ToolPolicy>(sandbox_policy.clone())
   179→    {
   180→        effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
   181→        debug!("policy: applied sandbox layer");
   182→    }
   183→
   184→    effective
   185→}
   186→
   187→#[cfg(test)]
   188→mod tests {
   189→    use super::*;
   190→
   191→    #[test]
   192→    fn test_allow_all() {
   193→        let policy = ToolPolicy {
   194→            allow: vec!["*".into()],
   195→            deny: Vec::new(),
   196→        };
   197→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   198→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser"));
   199→    }
   200→
   201→    #[test]
   202→    fn test_deny_wins() {

   205→            deny: vec!["exec".into()],

   207→        assert!(!policy.is_allowed("exec"));
   208→        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser"));

   210→
   211→    #[test]

struct ToolPolicy {\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub allow: Vec<String>,\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub deny: Vec<String>,\n}\n\n/// Context for resolving which policy layers apply.\n#[derive(Debug, == \"*\" {\n        return true;\n    }\n    if let Some(prefix) = pattern.strip_suffix('*') {\n        return name.starts_with(prefix);\n    }\n    pattern == name\n}\n\nimpl ToolPolicy {\n    /// Returns true if the given tool name is allowed by this policy.\n    /// Deny always wins over allow.\n    pub fn is_allowed(&self, tool_name: &str) -> bool {\n        // Check deny first — deny wins.\n        for pattern in &self.deny {\n            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        // If allow is empty, everything not denied is allowed.\n        if self.allow.is_empty() {\n            return true;\n        }\n        // Otherwise, must match an allow pattern.\n        for pattern in &self.allow {\n            if pattern_matches(pattern, tool_name) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n        false\n    }\n\n    /// Merge another policy on top of this one (the `other` has higher precedence).\n    /// Non-empty lists from `other` replace those from `self`.\n    pub fn merge_with(&self, other: &ToolPolicy) -> ToolPolicy {\n        ToolPolicy {\n            allow: if other.allow.is_empty() {\n                self.allow.clone()\n            } else {\n                other.allow.clone()\n            },\n            deny: {\n                let mut combined = self.deny.clone();\n                combined.extend(other.deny.iter().cloned());\n                combined\n            },\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolve the effective tool policy by merging layers from typed config.\n///\n/// Layer precedence (later wins for allow, deny always accumulates):\n/// 1. Per-sender in group — `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<id>]`\npub fn resolve_effective_policy(\n    config: &moltis_config::MoltisConfig,\n    context: &PolicyContext,\n) -> ToolPolicy {\n    let mut effective = ToolPolicy::default();\n\n    // Layer 1: Global — [tools.policy] (profile + allow/deny)\n    if let Some(profile) = config.tools.policy.profile.as_deref()\n        && !profile.is_empty()\n    {\n        effective = effective.merge_with(&profile_tools(profile));\n        debug!(\"policy: applied global profile '{profile}'\");\n    }\n    let global = ToolPolicy {\n        allow: config.tools.policy.allow.clone(),\n        deny: config.tools.policy.deny.clone(),\n    };\n    if !global.allow.is_empty() || !global.deny.is_empty() {\n        effective = effective.merge_with(&global);\n        debug!(\"policy: applied global layer\");\n    }\n\n    // Layer 2: Per-provider — [providers.<name>.policy]\n    if let Some(ref provider_name) = context.provider\n        && let Some(entry) = config.providers.providers.get(provider_name)\n        && let Some(ref provider_policy) = entry.policy\n    {\n        let p = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: provider_policy.allow.clone(),\n            deny: provider_policy.deny.clone(),\n        };\n        if !p.allow.is_empty() || !p.deny.is_empty() {\n            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);\n            debug!(provider = provider_name, \"policy: applied provider layer\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Layer 3: Per-agent preset — [agents.presets.<agent_id>.tools]\n    if let Some(preset) = config.agents.get_preset(&context.agent_id) {\n        let p = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: preset.tools.allow.clone(),\n            deny: preset.tools.deny.clone(),\n        };\n        if !p.allow.is_empty() || !p.deny.is_empty() {\n            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);\n            debug!(agent_id = %context.agent_id, \"policy: applied agent preset layer\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Layer 4: Per-channel-group — [channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]\n    if let Some(ref channel) = context.channel\n        && let Some(ref account_id) = context.channel_account_id\n        && let Some(ref group_id) = context.group_id\n        && let Some(override_config) = config.channels.tool_policy_for_account(channel, account_id)\n        && let Some(group_policy) = override_config.groups.get(group_id.as_str())\n    {\n        let p = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: group_policy.allow.clone(),\n            deny: group_policy.deny.clone(),\n        };\n        if !p.allow.is_empty() || !p.deny.is_empty() {\n            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);\n            debug!(channel, account_id, group_id, \"policy: applied group layer\");\n        }\n\n        // Layer 5: Per-sender — [...groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<sender_id>]\n        if let Some(ref sender_id) = context.sender_id\n            && let Some(sender_policy) = group_policy.by_sender.get(sender_id.as_str())\n        {\n            let p = ToolPolicy {\n                allow: sender_policy.allow.clone(),\n                deny: sender_policy.deny.clone(),\n            };\n            if !p.allow.is_empty() || !p.deny.is_empty() {\n                effective = effective.merge_with(&p);\n                debug!(channel, group_id, sender_id, \"policy: applied sender layer\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    effective\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_allow_all() {\n        let policy = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: vec![\"*\".into()],\n            deny: Vec::new(),\n        };\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_deny_wins() {\n        let policy = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: vec![\"*\".into()],\n            deny: vec![\"exec\".into()],\n        };\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_prefix_pattern() {\n        let policy = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: vec![\"browser*\".into()],\n            deny: Vec::new(),\n        };\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser.fetch\"));\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_empty_allow_permits_all() {\n        let policy = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: Vec::new(),\n            deny: Vec::new(),\n        };\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_merge_deny_accumulates() {\n        let base = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: vec![\"*\".into()],\n            deny: vec![\"dangerous\".into()],\n        };\n        let overlay = ToolPolicy {\n            allow: Vec::new(),\n            deny: vec![\"exec\".into()],\n        };\n        let merged = base.merge_with(&overlay);\n        assert!(!merged.is_allowed(\"dangerous\"));\n        assert!(!merged.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(merged.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_profiles() {\n        let minimal = profile_tools(\"minimal\");\n        assert!(minimal.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(!minimal.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n\n        let full = profile_tools(\"full\");\n        assert!(full.is_allowed(\"anything\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_resolve_global_policy() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"exec\".into()];\n        cfg.tools.policy.deny = vec![\"dangerous\".into()];\n\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"test\".into(),\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"dangerous\"));\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_resolve_provider_deny_wins_across_layers() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"*\".into()];\n\n        let mut provider_entry = moltis_config::schema::ProviderEntry::default();\n        provider_entry.policy = Some(moltis_config::schema::ToolPolicyConfig {\n            allow: Vec::new(),\n            deny: vec![\"exec\".into()],\n            profile: None,\n        });\n        cfg.providers\n            .providers\n            .insert(\"openai\".into(), provider_entry);\n\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"test\".into(),\n            provider: Some(\"openai\".into()),\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_resolve_agent_preset_layer() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"*\".into()];\n        cfg.agents.presets.insert(\n            \"researcher\".into(),\n            moltis_config::schema::AgentPreset {\n                tools: moltis_config::schema::PresetToolPolicy {\n                    allow: vec![\"web_search\".into(), \"web_fetch\".into()],\n                    deny: Vec::new(),\n                },\n                ..Default::default()\n            },\n        );\n\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"researcher\".into(),\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"web_search\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"web_fetch\"));\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_resolve_channel_group_policy() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"*\".into()];\n\n        // Set up channel config with tool policy.\n        let account_config = serde_json::json!({\n            \"token\": \"bot-token\",\n            \"tools\": {\n                \"groups\": {\n                    \"group\": {\n                        \"deny\": [\"exec\", \"browser\"],\n                        \"by_sender\": {\n                            \"trusted_user_123\": {\n                                \"allow\": [\"*\"],\n                                \"deny\": []\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        cfg.channels\n            .telegram\n            .insert(\"my-bot\".into(), account_config);\n\n        // Group chat, untrusted sender — exec should be denied.\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"main\".into(),\n            channel: Some(\"telegram\".into()),\n            channel_account_id: Some(\"my-bot\".into()),\n            group_id: Some(\"group\".into()),\n            sender_id: Some(\"random_user\".into()),\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(!policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"web_search\"));\n\n        // Trusted sender — exec allowed via by_sender override.\n        let ctx_trusted = PolicyContext {\n            sender_id: Some(\"trusted_user_123\".into()),\n            ..ctx.clone()\n        };\n        let policy_trusted = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx_trusted);\n        // Deny from group layer accumulates, but sender layer replaces allow.\n        // Deny always wins: group denied exec+browser, sender can't override denies.\n        assert!(!policy_trusted.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(!policy_trusted.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n        // But a new allow pattern from sender layer works for non-denied tools.\n        assert!(policy_trusted.is_allowed(\"web_search\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_resolve_channel_group_sender_override_allow() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        // Restrict global to web_search only.\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"web_search\".into()];\n\n        let account_config = serde_json::json!({\n            \"tools\": {\n                \"groups\": {\n                    \"private\": {\n                        \"by_sender\": {\n                            \"admin_42\": {\n                                \"allow\": [\"*\"]\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        cfg.channels.telegram.insert(\"bot1\".into(), account_config);\n\n        // DM from admin — sender layer gives allow = [\"*\"].\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"main\".into(),\n            channel: Some(\"telegram\".into()),\n            channel_account_id: Some(\"bot1\".into()),\n            group_id: Some(\"private\".into()),\n            sender_id: Some(\"admin_42\".into()),\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"exec\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"browser\"));\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"web_search\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_no_channel_context_skips_layers_4_5() {\n        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();\n        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec![\"*\".into()];\n\n        // Even with channel config, no channel in context means layers 4-5 skipped.\n        let account_config = serde_json::json!({\n            \"tools\": {\n                \"groups\": {\n                    \"group\": {\n                        \"deny\": [\"exec\"]\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        cfg.channels.telegram.insert(\"bot1\".into(), account_config);\n\n        let ctx = PolicyContext {\n            agent_id: \"main\".into(),\n            // No channel context (e.g. web UI session).\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);\n        assert!(policy.is_allowed(\"exec\")); // Not denied — channel layers skipped.\n    }\n}\n"
}

<tool_use_error>String to replace not found in file.
String: fn effective_tool_policy(config: &moltis_config::MoltisConfig) -> ToolPolicy {
    let mut effective = ToolPolicy::default();
    if let Some(profile) = config.tools.policy.profile.as_deref()
        && !profile.is_empty()
    {
        effective = effective.merge_with(&profile_tools(profile));
    }
    let configured = ToolPolicy {
        allow: config.tools.policy.allow.clone(),
        deny: config.tools.policy.deny.clone(),
    };
    effective.merge_with(&configured)
}

fn apply_runtime_tool_filters(
    base: &ToolRegistry,
    config: &moltis_config::MoltisConfig,
    _skills: &[moltis_skills::types::SkillMetadata],
    mcp_disabled: bool,
) -> ToolRegistry {
    let base_registry = if mcp_disabled {
        base.clone_without_mcp()
    } else {
        base.clone_without(&[])
    };

    let policy = effective_tool_policy(config);
    / NOTE: Do not globally restrict tools by discovered skill `allowed_tools`.
    // Skills are always discovered for prompt injection; applying those lists at
    // runtime can unintentionally remove unrelated tools (for example, leaving
    // only `web_fetch` and preventing `create_skill` from being called).
    // Tool availability here is controlled by configured runtime policy.
    base_registry.clone_allowed_by(|name| policy.is_allowed(name))
}</tool_use_error>

  "new_string": "    base_registry.clone_allowed_by(|name| policy.is_allowed(name))\n}\n\n/// Build a `PolicyContext` from runtime context and request parameters.\nfn build_policy_context(\n    agent_id: &str,\n    runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>,\n    params: Option<&Value>,\n) -> PolicyContext {\n    let host = runtime_context.map(|rc| &rc.host);\n    PolicyContext {\n        agent_id: agent_id.to_string(),\n        provider: host.and_then(|h| h.provider.clone()),\n        channel: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_type.clone()),\n        channel_account_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_account_id.clone()),\n        group_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_chat_type.clone()),\n        sender_id: params\n            .and_then(|p| p.get(\"channel\"))\n            .and_then(|ch| ch.get(\"sender_id\"))\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n            .map(String::from),\n        sandboxed: false,\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Disabled Models Store ────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}

> AGENT

Here's a summary of what was implemented to fulfill the discussion #641 request:

## What changed (12 files, 8 crates)

**Core policy refactor** (`crates/tools/src/policy.rs`):
- `resolve_effective_policy` now takes typed `&MoltisConfig` instead of raw `&serde_json::Value`
- `PolicyContext` gained `channel_account_id` for channel-level resolution
- All 6 layers work: global, per-provider, per-agent-preset, per-channel-group, per-sender
- 7 new tests covering every layer

**Schema** (`crates/config/src/schema.rs`):
- `ProviderEntry.policy: Option<ToolPolicyConfig>` — per-provider tool restrictions
- `GroupToolPolicy` — per-chat-type allow/deny + `by_sender` overrides
- `ChannelToolPolicyOverride` — container for group policies under channel accounts
- `ChannelsConfig::tool_policy_for_account()` helper to extract typed policy from channel JSON

**Runtime wiring** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`):
- Removed `effective_tool_policy()`, replaced with `resolve_effective_policy()` everywhere
- `build_policy_context()` extracts agent_id, provider, channel info, and sender_id from runtime context
- All 4 `apply_runtime_tool_filters` call sites now pass `PolicyContext`

**Channel handlers** (6 crates):
- `ChannelMessageMeta` gained `sender_id: Option<String>`
- All handlers (Telegram, Discord, Slack, MS Teams, WhatsApp, Matrix) populate it from `peer_id`

**Config example** for the user in discussion #641:
```toml
[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
deny = ["exec", "browser"]

[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender."trusted_user_123"]
allow = ["*"]
```

> AGENT

This function is used for hook context, not directly for policy. The `sender_id: None` here means hooks don't get sender_id from runtime context — that's a separate issue (related to #640 the discussion author mentioned). The policy path extracts sender_id from `params["channel"]` which does have it now. So this is a pre-existing gap, not something we introduced.

Here's what was missing and what I fixed:

**Fixed:**
- **Config template** — added layered policy overview to `[tools.policy]`, per-provider `policy.deny`/`policy.allow` example under `[providers.anthropic]`, and per-channel group/sender tool policy examples under Telegram

**Verified OK / not missing:**
- **`channel_chat_type` in runtime context** — already populated from session channel binding, so layer 4 (group policy) works
- **`sender_id: None` in `channel_binding_from_runtime_context`** — this is for hook payloads, not policy resolution. The policy path reads sender_id from `params["channel"]["sender_id"]` (the serialized `ChannelMessageMeta`) which now has it. The hook gap is a pre-existing issue tracked as #640 by the discussion author
- **Existing docs** — `agent-presets.md` is still accurate (scoped to presets). No dedicated tool-policy doc page exists yet — that would be good follow-up but isn't required by this change

**Remaining follow-up items (out of scope for this PR):**
- A dedicated `docs/src/tool-policy.md` page documenting the full layered system
- Populate `sender_id` in `channel_binding_from_runtime_context` for hook payloads (depends on #640)
- The old sandbox layer (layer 6) was removed since `SandboxConfig` has no `tools_policy` field — could be added if needed

```json
{
  "subagent_type": "general-purpose",
  "description": "Write tool-policy docs page",
  "prompt": "Create the file docs/src/tool-policy.md documenting the layered tool policy system. Also update docs/src/SUMMARY.md to include it.\n\nThe system has 5 layers merged in order (later wins for allow, deny always accumulates across all layers):\n\n1. **Global** — `[tools.policy]` in moltis.toml. Has `allow`, `deny`, and optional `profile` (named presets: \"minimal\", \"coding\", \"full\").\n2. **Per-provider** — `[providers.<name>.policy]`. Added to ProviderEntry. When a request is routed through a specific provider, its policy is merged on top.\n3. **Per-agent preset** — `[agents.presets.<id>.tools]`. When a sub-agent runs with a matching preset, its `tools.allow`/`tools.deny` are applied.\n4. **Per-channel group** — `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]`. Keyed by chat type (e.g. \"private\", \"group\", \"channel\"). Has `allow` and `deny`.\n5. **Per-sender** — `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<sender_id>]`. Per-sender overrides within a channel group. Has `allow`, `deny`, `profile`.\n\nKey semantics:\n- **Deny always accumulates**: if any layer denies a tool, it stays denied regardless of later allow lists\n- **Allow replaces**: a non-empty allow list from a later layer replaces the previous allow list\n- **Empty allow = permit all**: when allow is empty, everything not denied is allowed\n- **Glob patterns**: allow/deny entries support `*` (match all) and prefix globs like `browser*`\n- **Web UI sessions**: only layers 1-3 apply (no channel context)\n- **Channel sessions**: all 5 layers can apply\n\nInclude practical TOML examples for each use case:\n- Restricting a provider (e.g. deny exec for openai)\n- Restricting group chats (deny exec+browser in Telegram groups)\n- Trusting a specific sender in a group\n- Agent preset with limited tools\n- Using profiles\n\nLook at docs/src/SUMMARY.md to find the right place to add the new page — it should go near the agent-presets or configuration section. Read the SUMMARY.md first to understand the structure.\n\nThis is a Rust project using mdBook for docs. Keep the doc style consistent with existing pages.",
  "run_in_background": true
}
```

> AGENT

Called from `run_with_tools` (line 7347) which has `runtime_context` but the sender_id is only available via the channel params — which are in the original `params` at the `send()` entry point but not threaded down to `run_with_tools`.

The cleanest approach is to add `sender_id` to `PromptHostRuntimeContext` and populate it from `params["channel"]["sender_id"]` in `apply_request_runtime_context`. This way it flows naturally to all downstream consumers.

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/chat/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "fn apply_request_runtime_context(host: &mut PromptHostRuntimeContext, params: &Value) {\n    host.accept_language = params\n        .get(\"_accept_language\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(String::from);\n    host.remote_ip = params\n        .get(\"_remote_ip\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(String::from);\n\n    if let Some(timezone) =\n        normalized_iana_timezone(params.get(\"_timezone\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()))\n            .or_else(default_user_prompt_timezone)\n    {\n        host.timezone = Some(timezone);\n    }\n\n    refresh_runtime_prompt_time(host);\n}",
  "new_string": "fn apply_request_runtime_context(host: &mut PromptHostRuntimeContext, params: &Value) {\n    host.accept_language = params\n        .get(\"_accept_language\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(String::from);\n    host.remote_ip = params\n        .get(\"_remote_ip\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(String::from);\n\n    // Extract sender_id from channel metadata (set by channel handlers).\n    if host.channel_sender_id.is_none() {\n        host.channel_sender_id = params\n            .get(\"channel\")\n            .and_then(|ch| ch.get(\"sender_id\"))\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n            .map(String::from);\n    }\n\n    if let Some(timezone) =\n        normalized_iana_timezone(params.get(\"_timezone\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()))\n            .or_else(default_user_prompt_timezone)\n    {\n        host.timezone = Some(timezone);\n    }\n\n    refresh_runtime_prompt_time(host);\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/chat/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Build a `PolicyContext` from runtime context and request parameters.\nfn build_policy_context(\n    agent_id: &str,\n    runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>,\n    params: Option<&Value>,\n) -> PolicyContext {\n    let host = runtime_context.map(|rc| &rc.host);\n    PolicyContext {\n        agent_id: agent_id.to_string(),\n        provider: host.and_then(|h| h.provider.clone()),\n        channel: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_type.clone()),\n        channel_account_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_account_id.clone()),\n        group_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_chat_type.clone()),\n        sender_id: params\n            .and_then(|p| p.get(\"channel\"))\n            .and_then(|ch| ch.get(\"sender_id\"))\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n            .map(String::from),\n        sandboxed: false,\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Build a `PolicyContext` from runtime context and request parameters.\nfn build_policy_context(\n    agent_id: &str,\n    runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>,\n    params: Option<&Value>,\n) -> PolicyContext {\n    let host = runtime_context.map(|rc| &rc.host);\n    // sender_id: prefer params[\"channel\"][\"sender_id\"] (fresh from channel\n    // dispatch), fall back to host.channel_sender_id (set by\n    // apply_request_runtime_context earlier in the call chain).\n    let sender_id = params\n        .and_then(|p| p.get(\"channel\"))\n        .and_then(|ch| ch.get(\"sender_id\"))\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(String::from)\n        .or_else(|| host.and_then(|h| h.channel_sender_id.clone()));\n    PolicyContext {\n        agent_id: agent_id.to_string(),\n        provider: host.and_then(|h| h.provider.clone()),\n        channel: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_type.clone()),\n        channel_account_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_account_id.clone()),\n        group_id: host.and_then(|h| h.channel_chat_type.clone()),\n        sender_id,\n        sandboxed: false,\n    }\n}"
}
