```
     1→# Tool Policy
     2→
     3→Tool policies control which tools are available during a session. Policies
     4→use a layered system where each layer can restrict or widen access, and
     5→**deny always wins** — once a tool is denied at any layer, no later layer
     6→can re-allow it.
     7→
     8→## Layers
     9→
    10→Five layers are evaluated in order. Later layers can replace the allow list,
    11→but deny entries accumulate across all of them.
    12→
    13→| # | Layer | Config path | Applies to |
    14→|---|-------|-------------|------------|
    15→| 1 | Global | `[tools.policy]` | All sessions |
    16→| 2 | Per-provider | `[providers.<name>.policy]` | Requests routed through that provider |
    17→| 3 | Per-agent preset | `[agents.presets.<id>.tools]` | Sub-agents spawned with that preset |
    18→| 4 | Per-channel group | `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]` | Channel sessions matching that chat type |
    19→| 5 | Per-sender | `...groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<sender_id>` | Messages from that sender in that group |
    20→
    21→**Web UI sessions** only see layers 1-3 (no channel context).
    22→**Channel sessions** can see all 5 layers.
    23→
    24→## Merge Semantics
    25→
    26→Each layer produces an `allow` list and a `deny` list. When merging a
    27→higher-priority layer on top of a lower one:
    28→
    29→- **Deny accumulates.** Every deny entry from every layer is collected. If
    30→  any layer denies a tool, it stays denied.
    31→- **Allow replaces.** A non-empty allow list from a later layer replaces the
    32→  previous allow list entirely. An empty allow list is a no-op (keeps the
    33→  previous allow list).
    34→- **Empty allow = permit all.** When the effective allow list is empty,
    35→  everything not denied is allowed.
    36→
    37→## Glob Patterns
    38→
    39→Both `allow` and `deny` entries support glob-style patterns:
    40→
    41→- `"*"` — matches every tool name
    42→- `"browser*"` — matches any tool whose name starts with `browser`
    43→- `"exec"` — matches only the exact tool name `exec`
    44→
    45→## Profiles
    46→
    47→The global layer and per-sender overrides support a `profile` field that
    48→expands to a predefined allow list before the explicit allow/deny entries
    49→are applied.
    50→
```

id: toolu_01FJ3v4wNR8xh8LdrD8Pwkbd
```
    50→
    51→| Profile | Allow list |
    52→|---------|-----------|
    53→| `"minimal"` | `exec` |
    54→| `"coding"` | `exec`, `browser`, `memory` |
    55→| `"full"` | `*` (everything) |
    56→
    57→When a profile is set, its allow list is applied first, then the explicit
    58→`allow`/`deny` from the same layer are merged on top (deny accumulates,
    59→non-empty allow replaces).
    60→
    61→## Layer 1 — Global
    62→
    63→The base policy for all sessions. Set in `moltis.toml` under `[tools.policy]`:
    64→
    65→```toml
    66→[tools.policy]
    67→allow = []           # empty = permit all tools not denied
    68→deny = ["browser"]   # deny browser in every session
    69→# profile = "full"   # optional named profile
    70→```
    71→
    72→## Layer 2 — Per-Provider
    73→
    74→Each provider entry can carry its own policy. When a request is routed
    75→through that provider, the policy is merged on top of the global layer.
    76→
    77→```toml
    78→[providers.openai]
    79→# ... api_key, models, etc.
    80→policy.deny = ["exec"]
    81→```
    82→
    83→This denies `exec` whenever OpenAI is the active provider, regardless of
    84→what the global layer allows. Other providers are unaffected.
    85→
    86→## Layer 3 — Per-Agent Preset
    87→
    88→Agent presets (used by `spawn_agent`) can restrict their sub-agent's tools.
    89→
    90→```toml
    91→[agents.presets.researcher]
    92→model = "anthropic/claude-haiku-3-5-20241022"
    93→tools.allow = ["read_file", "glob", "grep", "web_search", "web_fetch"]
    94→tools.deny  = ["exec", "write_file"]
    95→```
    96→
    97→When the `researcher` preset is active, only the five listed tools are
    98→allowed, and `exec`/`write_file` are explicitly denied. See
    99→[Agent Presets](agent-presets.md) for the full preset reference.
   100→
   101→> **Note:** Preset tool policies apply only to sub-agents spawned via
   102→> `spawn_agent`. They do not affect the main agent session. Use the global
   103→> `[tools.policy]` for the main session.
   104→
   105→## Layer 4 — Per-Channel Group
   106→
   107→Channel accounts can restrict tools by chat type (`private`, `group`,
   108→`channel`, etc.). This is useful for hardening group chats where the bot
   109→is exposed to untrusted users.
   110→
   111→```toml
   112→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   113→deny = ["exec", "browser"]
   114→```
   115→
   116→In this example, `exec` and `browser` are denied in Telegram group chats
   117→handled by the `my-bot` account. Private chats and web UI sessions are
   118→unaffected.
   119→
   120→## Layer 5 — Per-Sender
   121→
   122→Within a channel group, individual senders can receive overrides. This lets
   123→you trust specific users in an otherwise restricted group.
   124→
   125→```toml
   126→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   127→deny = ["exec", "browser"]
   128→
   129→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender."123456"]
   130→allow = ["*"]
   131→```
   132→
   133→Sender `123456` gets `allow = ["*"]`, which replaces the previous allow
   134→list. However, because **deny always accumulates**, the `exec` and
   135→`browser` denials from the group layer still apply. The sender override
   136→is useful for widening the allow list (e.g., granting access to tools that
   137→were not in the previous allow set) or for applying a different profile.
   138→
   139→If you need a trusted sender to have `exec` access in a group, avoid
   140→denying `exec` at the group layer. Instead, use a restrictive allow list
   141→at the group level and widen it per-sender:
   142→
   143→```toml
   144→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   145→allow = ["web_search", "web_fetch", "memory_search"]
   146→
   147→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender."123456"]
   148→allow = ["*"]
   149→```
   150→
   151→Here, untrusted group members can only use the three listed tools. Sender
   152→`123456` gets full access because the group layer did not deny anything —
   153→it only narrowed the allow list.
   154→
   155→## Examples
   156→
   157→### Deny exec for a specific provider
   158→
   159→```toml
   160→[providers.openai]
   161→policy.deny = ["exec"]
   162→```
   163→
   164→When using OpenAI, the agent cannot run shell commands. All other
   165→providers retain their normal tool access.
   166→
   167→### Restrict group chats on Telegram
   168→
   169→```toml
   170→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   171→deny = ["exec", "browser*"]
   172→```
   173→
   174→Group chats cannot use `exec` or any tool starting with `browser`.
   175→Private chats are unaffected.
   176→
   177→### Trust a sender in a restricted group
   178→
   179→```toml
   180→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   181→allow = ["web_search", "web_fetch"]
   182→
   183→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender."123456"]
   184→allow = ["*"]
   185→```
   186→
   187→Normal group members can only search and fetch. Sender `123456` can use
   188→every tool (nothing was denied at the group layer, so nothing accumulates).
   189→
   190→### Agent preset with limited tools
   191→
   192→```toml
   193→[agents.presets.researcher]
   194→tools.allow = ["read_file", "glob", "grep"]
   195→tools.deny  = ["exec"]
   196→```
   197→
   198→The `researcher` sub-agent can only read files and search. Even if a
   199→higher layer allows `exec`, it is denied here and the denial carries
   200→through.
   201→
   202→### Use a profile for the global policy
   203→
   204→```toml
   205→[tools.policy]
   206→profile = "coding"
   207→deny = ["web_fetch"]
   208→```
   209→
   210→The `coding` profile expands to `allow = ["exec", "browser", "memory"]`.
   211→Then `web_fetch` is denied. The effective policy allows `exec`, `browser`,
   212→and `memory`, and denies `web_fetch`. All other tools are not in the allow
   213→list and are therefore blocked.
   214→
   215→### Widen a sender via profile
   216→
   217→```toml
   218→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]
   219→allow = ["web_search"]
   220→
   221→[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender."123456"]
   222→profile = "full"
   223→```
   224→
   225→Sender `123456` gets `allow = ["*"]` from the `full` profile, replacing
   226→the group's narrow allow list. Since the group layer only set `allow` (no
   227→`deny`), nothing is denied and the sender has full tool access.
   228→
   229→## Debugging
   230→
   231→Enable `debug` logging to see which layers are applied at runtime:
   232→
   233→```
   234→policy: applied global profile 'coding'
   235→policy: applied global layer
   236→policy: applied provider layer    provider=openai
   237→policy: applied agent preset layer agent_id=researcher
   238→policy: applied group layer       channel=telegram account_id=my-bot group_id=group
   239→policy: applied sender layer      channel=telegram group_id=group sender_id=123456
   240→```
   241→
   242→Each line indicates a layer was non-empty and merged into the effective
   243→policy. Missing lines mean that layer had no configuration or the runtime
   244→context did not match (e.g., no channel context for a web UI session).
   245→
```

@@ -103,6 +103,7 @@ impl ToolPolicy {
 /// 3. Per-agent preset — `[agents.presets.<agent_id>.tools]`
 /// 4. Per-channel-group — `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]`
 /// 5. Per-sender in group — `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<id>]`
+/// 6. Sandbox overrides — `[tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]` (only when `context.sandboxed`)
 pub fn resolve_effective_policy(
     config: &moltis_config::MoltisConfig,
     context: &PolicyContext,
@@ -183,6 +184,20 @@ pub fn resolve_effective_policy(
         }
     }
 
+    // Layer 6: Sandbox overrides — [tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]
+    if context.sandboxed
+        && let Some(ref sandbox_policy) = config.tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy
+    {
+        let p = ToolPolicy {
+            allow: sandbox_policy.allow.clone(),
+            deny: sandbox_policy.deny.clone(),
+        };
+        if !p.allow.is_empty() || !p.deny.is_empty() {
+            effective = effective.merge_with(&p);
+            debug!("policy: applied sandbox layer");
+        }
+    }
+
     effective
 }
 
@@ -436,4 +451,38 @@ mod tests {
         let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);
         assert!(policy.is_allowed("exec")); // Not denied — channel layers skipped.
     }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn test_resolve_sandbox_layer_overrides() {
+        let mut cfg = moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default();
+        cfg.tools.policy.allow = vec!["*".into()];
+
+        // Configure sandbox-specific tool policy that denies browser.
+        cfg.tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy = Some(moltis_config::schema::ToolPolicyConfig {
+            allow: vec!["exec".into()],
+            deny: vec!["browser".into()],
+            profile: None,
+        });
+
+        // Without sandboxed flag — layer 6 is skipped.
+        let ctx = PolicyContext {
+            agent_id: "main".into(),
+            sandboxed: false,
+            ..Default::default()
+        };
+        let policy = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx);
+        assert!(policy.is_allowed("exec"));
+        assert!(policy.is_allowed("browser")); // Not denied — sandbox layer skipped.
+
+        // With sandboxed flag — layer 6 applies.
+        let ctx_sandboxed = PolicyContext {
+            agent_id: "main".into(),
+            sandboxed: true,
+            ..Default::default()
+        };
+        let policy_sandboxed = resolve_effective_policy(&cfg, &ctx_sandboxed);
+        assert!(policy_sandboxed.is_allowed("exec"));
+        assert!(!policy_sandboxed.is_allowed("browser")); // Denied by sandbox layer.
+        assert!(!policy_sandboxed.is_allowed("web_search")); // Not in sandbox allow list.
+    }
 }

  "new_string": "Six layers are evaluated in order. Later layers can replace the allow list,\nbut deny entries accumulate across all of them.\n\n| # | Layer | Config path | Applies to |\n|---|-------|-------------|------------|\n| 1 | Global | `[tools.policy]` | All sessions |\n| 2 | Per-provider | `[providers.<name>.policy]` | Requests routed through that provider |\n| 3 | Per-agent preset | `[agents.presets.<id>.tools]` | Sub-agents spawned with that preset |\n| 4 | Per-channel group | `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]` | Channel sessions matching that chat type |\n| 5 | Per-sender | `...groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<sender_id>` | Messages from that sender in that group |\n| 6 | Sandbox | `[tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]` | Commands running inside a sandbox container |\n\n**Web UI sessions** see layers 1-3 (no channel context), plus layer 6 if sandboxed.\n**Channel sessions** can see all 6 layers."
}

It lets you restrict tools for sandboxed execution without\naffecting non-sandboxed sessions.\n\n```toml\n[tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]\nallow = [\"exec\"]         # only exec inside sandbox\ndeny = [\"browser\"]       # never allow browser in sandbox\n```\n\nThis layer is skipped entirely when the session is not sandboxed.\n\n## Examples"
}

> AGENT

Yes — `sandboxed: false` is hardcoded. The sandbox state is available in `PromptRuntimeContext.sandbox.exec_sandboxed`. Let me fix this.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01VomFDsNqs446AKBnDaY3yC
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/curious-bill/crates/chat/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "        sender_id,\n        sandboxed: false,\n    }",
  "new_string": "        sender_id,\n        sandboxed: runtime_context\n            .and_then(|rc| rc.sandbox.as_ref())\n            .is_some_and(|s| s.exec_sandboxed),\n    }"
}
```

{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"feat(tools): wire layered tool policy into runtime (6 layers)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- **Wire `resolve_effective_policy` into the runtime**, replacing the simple `effective_tool_policy()` that only read global config. All 4 `apply_runtime_tool_filters` call sites now pass a `PolicyContext` built from runtime context.\n- **6-layer policy resolution**: global → per-provider → per-agent-preset → per-channel-group → per-sender → sandbox. Deny accumulates across layers; allow replaces.\n- **New config fields**: `ProviderEntry.policy`, `GroupToolPolicy`, `ChannelToolPolicyOverride`, `SandboxConfig.tools_policy`\n- **`sender_id` threaded through all 6 channel handlers** (Telegram, Discord, Slack, MS Teams, WhatsApp, Matrix) into `ChannelMessageMeta`, `PromptHostRuntimeContext`, and hook payloads\n- **Documentation**: new `docs/src/tool-policy.md` with full layer reference, merge semantics, TOML examples, and debugging guide\n\nCloses the feature request in discussion #641.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Completed\n- [x] `cargo check` — zero warnings\n- [x] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 fmt --all -- --check`\n- [x] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 clippy -p moltis-config -p moltis-tools -p moltis-chat -p moltis-channels -p moltis-telegram -p moltis-discord -p moltis-slack -p moltis-msteams -p moltis-whatsapp -p moltis-matrix -- -D warnings`\n- [x] `cargo test -p moltis-config -p moltis-tools -p moltis-chat -p moltis-channels` — 1,278 tests pass\n- [x] 14 policy-specific tests covering all 6 layers\n- [x] mdBook builds clean (`cd docs && mdbook build`)\n\n### Remaining\n- [ ] `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR_NUMBER>`\n- [ ] Full `just test`\n\n## Manual QA\n\n1. Add `policy.deny = [\"exec\"]` under `[providers.openai]` in `moltis.toml` — verify `exec` tool is unavailable when using OpenAI but available with other providers\n2. Add channel tool policy under `[channels.telegram.<bot>.tools.groups.group]` with `deny = [\"exec\"]` — verify exec is blocked in group chats but allowed in DMs\n3. Add `[tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]` with `deny = [\"browser\"]` — verify browser tool is blocked when sandbox is active\n4. Check `RUST_LOG=debug` output shows `policy: applied <layer> layer` traces\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create PR",
  "timeout": 30000
}

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_017bo5BiibVLLq3UoVECbPRy
```json
{
  "command": "gh api repos/moltis-org/moltis/discussions/641/comments -f body=\"$(cat <<'EOF'\nThis is now implemented in #677. Here's what landed:\n\n**The answer is: yes, this is still the intended direction — and it's now wired up.**\n\n`resolve_effective_policy` has been refactored from raw JSON pointer walks to typed `MoltisConfig`, connected to the runtime at all 4 `apply_runtime_tool_filters` call sites, and extended to 6 layers:\n\n| # | Layer | Config path |\n|---|-------|-------------|\n| 1 | Global | `[tools.policy]` |\n| 2 | Per-provider | `[providers.<name>.policy]` |\n| 3 | Per-agent preset | `[agents.presets.<id>.tools]` |\n| 4 | Per-channel group | `[channels.<type>.<account>.tools.groups.<chat_type>]` |\n| 5 | Per-sender | `...groups.<chat_type>.by_sender.<sender_id>` |\n| 6 | Sandbox | `[tools.exec.sandbox.tools_policy]` |\n\nThe schema drift you identified is fixed — layer 3 now reads from `agents.presets` (not `agents.list`), and layers 4-5 use typed `GroupToolPolicy` / `ChannelToolPolicyOverride` structs with a `tool_policy_for_account()` helper that deserializes the `tools` sub-key from the channel account JSON on demand.\n\nFor your multi-channel use case with varying trust levels, you can now do:\n\n```toml\n# Restrict group chats\n[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group]\ndeny = [\"exec\", \"browser\"]\n\n# Trust a specific sender in groups\n[channels.telegram.my-bot.tools.groups.group.by_sender.\"123456\"]\nallow = [\"*\"]\n\n# DMs get full access (no restrictions needed — layers 4-5 only match when group_id matches)\n```\n\n`sender_id` is now threaded through `ChannelMessageMeta` from `peer_id` in all 6 channel handlers (Telegram, Discord, Slack, MS Teams, WhatsApp, Matrix), and flows into both `PolicyContext` for tool filtering and `ChannelBinding` for hook payloads — which partially addresses #640 as well.\n\nFull docs at `docs/src/tool-policy.md` in the PR.\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Post comment on discussion #641"
}
```
